# Sphere-pad contact model: assessment, experiment plan and documentation plan (2026-10-14)

Working notes of window 88ae0ebc, written from the mapping workflow wf_14df30c9-90c (seven readers, three experiment proposers, an assessor, a judge). Results go to docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/ and its HTML page; this file is the plan and the reference material the experiments and the overview revision use. Owner direction (2026-10-14): the pads' selling point is CPU and GPU usability; no studies that try to separate the pads from Newton hydroelastic.

## Experiment plan

### U1. Per-step cost of the fingertip contact models on the real_v1 hand on CPU threads, in MuJoCo-Warp batches and in PPO training (order 1, about 1.4 h)

**Hypothesis.** The 1 mm pads' CPU cost at the three-finger grip (105.5 µs per 1 ms step: 66.4 µs position stage, 38.2 µs solve; 85 contacts, 254 rows) comes from collision of 3,177 pad geoms against every geom and from a long solve. Pads that collide with the cylinder only, solved at the GPU planner's settings (tolerance 1e-6, 20 iterations, 10 line-search iterations), step the grip in at most 50 µs; 2 mm pads (870 geoms) in at most 35 µs; condim 4 on the TPU block in at most 16 µs (point contact: 13.4 µs). On the GPU at the servo plant's held RL state, 2 mm pads and condim 4 cost within 1.2 times the TPU mesh per world-step at 2,048 worlds. A 12-iteration PPO probe reproduces the 1 mm pads' 1.47 times iteration cost (8.07 against 5.50 s) within 10 %, and 2 mm pads and condim 4 run within 1.15 times the mesh. GPU memory follows the buffer rule of env_build._contact_buffers, not the contact count: every scene with more than 500 geoms gets 640 contacts, 3,072 rows and 768 sensor matches per world, so the 2 mm pad probe peaks within 15 % of the 1 mm pads' 12.5 GB.

**Falsifier.** Cylinder-only pads at the GPU solver settings above 70 µs per grip step (per-geom kinematics of 3,177 geoms sets the cost, and only fewer pads help); 2 mm pads or condim 4 above 1.3 times the mesh per PPO iteration (iteration cost does not follow the contact count); the 1 mm pad probe's ratio to the mesh outside 1.32-1.62 (12-iteration probes do not represent training, and the 40 M-step logs stay the cost measure).

**Decision informed.** Which planner models enter U4's closed loop (a configuration enters if its 64-plan replanning takes at most a third of the pads planner's at the same thread count; 0.83 s at 6 threads now); which servo-plant RL scenes U5 evaluates and the owner may train on; whether env_build._contact_buffers gets exact per-scene overrides to cut pad training memory; the hand rows of the overview's cost table.

**Protocol.** 1. CPU timing. New scripts/pad_usability_timing.py (about 120 lines) imports hand_object_scale_mpc_timing (step_split, rollout_time) and hand_object_scale_mpc.setup without editing them. Plant kp0.72_kv0_fr0.18_fl0_dp0.0144; cells cylinder:16:0.625 and cylinder:25:0.125; states open (after reset) and grip (0.8 s after the grip command). Models: pads (the plant scene); pads_f (pads collide with the cylinder only, as Planner's pad_filter: pads contype 2, conaffinity 0, cylinder conaffinity | 2); pads_fs (pads_f with opt.tolerance 1e-6, iterations 20, ls_iterations 10); pads2 (cell scene rebuilt with reorient_backends.replace_tips(s=0.002, rs=0.0015) and written as <tag>_pads2__<plant>.xml beside the cached scenes, never over <tag>_pads); pads2_f; pt; pt4 (pt with condim 4, priority 1 and friction '1 0.0028 0.0001' on each {f}_tipgeom; 2.8 mm is the pads' onset arm 2.527 F^0.070 mm from docs/experiments/20261008-contact_model_policies/twist_onset/tip_T2.jsonl at the cells' 3.5-7 N per finger); ptmc (pt with mjENBL_MULTICCD). Per model and state: step_split (absolute times, no callback), a second pass with mujoco.set_mjcb_time(time.perf_counter) for the stage shares of d.timer (POS_KINEMATICS, POS_INERTIA, COL_BROAD, COL_NARROW, POS_MAKE, POS_PROJECT, CONSTRAINT), ncon and nefc, and rollout_time of 64 plans x 0.5 s at 1 and 3 threads while the owner's planner jobs run (6 and 12 afterwards). RSS first: /usr/bin/time -v .venv/bin/python scripts/pad_usability_timing.py --cells cylinder:16:0.625 --models pads --threads 1 --out logs/20261014-pad_usability/rss_timing.jsonl. Run: ~/.claude/bin/resguard.sh run --mem 2G --cpu 400 -- taskset -c 17-19 .venv/bin/python scripts/pad_usability_timing.py --plant kp0.72_kv0_fr0.18_fl0_dp0.0144 --cells cylinder:16:0.625 cylinder:25:0.125 --models pads pads_f pads_fs pads2 pads2_f pt pt4 ptmc --threads 1 3 --out docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/cost/cpu_timing.jsonl.
2. Servo-plant RL scenes. uv run --extra rl python scripts/make_pad_morphology_run.py --src results/phase1/real_v1/20261011-sv1_u0308_b050_servo_tip --out results/phase1/real_v1/20261014-sv1_u0308_b050_servo_tip_tpu2.7pads2 --spacing 0.002 (radius defaults to 0.75 x spacing = 1.5 mm; pads touch the tool only). New scripts/pad_usability_rl.py scenes sets make_work_plant_runs.OUT = results/phase1/real_v1/20261011-sv1_u0308_b050_servo_tip and C4_DIR = results/phase1/real_v1/20261014-sv1_u0308_b050_servo_tip_tpu2.7mesh_c4, calls condim4_variant(spin=0.0029) (2.9 mm: the onset arm at the policies' 5-8 N per finger) and check([C4_DIR, the pads2 run]) at 64 envs (held worlds and tip contacts at policy steps 60 and 150); GPU, MUJOCO_GL=egl, its own WARP_CACHE_PATH.
3. GPU same-state cost. New scripts/pad_usability_gpu_cost.py (about 80 lines) adds servo_mesh, servo_meshc4, servo_pads2 and servo_pads1 to rl_contact_throughput.RUNS, exports each held state with rl_state_export.export(variant, 64, 60) into logs/20261014-pad_usability/state_<v>/ (rl_state_export.OUT patched), writes scene_key.xml with world 0's qpos and ctrl as keyframe 0, and times each variant with same_state_timing.run_mjw (STATES and OPTS patched) at 1,024, 2,048 and 4,096 worlds (8,192 where it fits), nconmax and njmax at 1.5 x the exported per-world maxima, one fresh process per point, two repeats at 2,048; a point whose memory estimate exceeds free VRAM minus 2 GB is recorded as skipped. Commands: MUJOCO_GL=egl WARP_CACHE_PATH=$(mktemp -d) ~/.claude/bin/resguard.sh run --mem 6G --cpu 200 -- taskset -c 16 uv run --extra rl --extra gpu python scripts/pad_usability_gpu_cost.py export --variants servo_mesh servo_meshc4 servo_pads2 servo_pads1; then the same prefix with pad_usability_gpu_cost.py time --variant <v> --nworld <n>. Kernel split: .venv/bin/mjwarp-testspeed logs/20261014-pad_usability/state_<v>/scene_key.xml --keyframe 0 --nworld 2048 --nstep 200 --event_trace --measure_alloc --memory --nconmax <n> --njmax <j> for the four variants (the exported scene.xml already contains the trainer's options).
4. PPO probes, smallest memory first (mesh, meshc4, pads2, pads1; pads1 and mesh twice), each the 2026-10-11 queue's trainer command cut to 12 iterations: MUJOCO_GL=egl WARP_CACHE_PATH=$(mktemp -d) ~/.claude/bin/resguard.sh run --mem 6G --cpu 300 -- taskset -c 16-18 /usr/bin/time -v uv run --extra rl --extra gpu python scripts/rl_train_cube.py --morphology-run <run> --recipe b_liveA --tag 20261014-probe_<v> --output-root logs/20261014-pad_usability/rl_probes --num-envs 2048 --total-timesteps 589824 --save-interval 41 --no-wandb --no-record-videos --closed-ctrl-from-keyframe open_ik --open-finger-from-keyframe --lift-target-z-above-init 0.1 --lift-delta-z 0.1 --finger-residual-scale 0.5 --finger-close-easing ease_out_quad --lift-phase-start-step 58 --reorient-start-step 58 --finger-residual-active-from-step 58 --term-tip-lost-steps 15 --friction-dr --grip-force-penalty-weight -2, with nvidia-smi --query-gpu=memory.used sampled every 2 s into logs/20261014-pad_usability/rl_probes/gpu_<v>.tsv. A probe whose log contains 'overflow' is discarded. If the pads2 probe peaks above 8 GB, add exact overrides MORPHOHAND_NCONMAX_SET, MORPHOHAND_NJMAX_SET and MORPHOHAND_MAXMATCH_SET to env_build._contact_buffers (unset leaves training unchanged; about 8 lines) and repeat the pads2 and pads1 probes at 1.5 x the exported maxima.
Checked against the repository: hand_object_scale_mpc_timing.py has no --configs and a fixed output path; rl_train_cube.py (tyro) has no --sim-timestep; env_build reads only MORPHOHAND_NCONMAX and only to raise the buffer; rl_state_export.py has no --keyframe and takes variants from rl_contact_throughput.RUNS (calibrated-plant runs only); same_state_timing.py accepts --variant legacy, mesh or pads1 only; mjwarp-testspeed takes a keyframe index.

**Samples and statistics.** CPU: 500 steps x 3 repeats per model, state and cell; median and range of µs per step and of each stage share; rollout timing 3 repeats per thread count. GPU: one fresh-process run per variant and batch, two at 2,048 worlds; µs per world-step, contacts and rows per world, held fraction, GPU MB. PPO: median and interquartile range of 'Iteration time' over iterations 3-12 per probe, peak GPU MB, host RSS; the 1 mm pad and mesh probes run twice; probe ratios are compared with the 40 M-step medians (8.07 and 5.50 s); differences under 10 % are not acted on (the 40 M-step logs vary about 4 % between deciles).

**Compute.** CPU part first: one process on three cores, RSS measured first (about 1.5 GB), about 20 min. GPU part afterwards, one Warp process at a time, each with its own WARP_CACHE_PATH, at least 2 GB of VRAM left free, host RSS 3-6 GB, one or three cores: scenes 5 min, exports 8 min, timing about 25 min, testspeed 8 min, probes about 25 min (the 1 mm pad probe needs 13.5 GB free and runs last). While the owner's planner jobs run, our CPU and GPU processes together stay within 4 cores. Quote ~/.claude/bin/resguard.sh status before each launch.

**Depends on.** None. Its scenes and exported states feed U3 and U5, and its replanning times gate U4's configurations.

**Films.** A three-panel clip of the grip on cylinder:16:0.625 with 1 mm pads, 2 mm pads and condim 4 on the block (the 0.8 s grip and 1 s after it, pads touching the cylinder drawn red, label bar with contacts and µs per step), 640x480 panels tiled with reorient_backends_films.tile_stream into docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/cost/media/; poster frame checked at its displayed size.

### U2. Agreement of the 1 mm pads and four cheaper fingertip models with Drake on the servo-limited plant (order 2, about 0.8 h)

**Hypothesis.** On the servo-limited plant (kp 0.72 N m/rad, 0.18 N m) the 1 mm pads end the eight deployed open-loop plans within 3 deg of Drake hydroelastic, keeping the tool, on at least 20 of 24 placements (23 of 24 on the 1 N m working plant), and on at least 18 of 24 at the trainer's numerics (impratio 10, 2 ms, 10 iterations). Tool-only pads, 2 mm pads and condim 4 on the block (μ_t 2.8 mm) reach at least 20 of 24 on both plants, while point contact (11 of 24 on the working plant) and multiccd stay below 20 of 24 on at least one plant. HOM closed loop ends within 5 deg of its Drake run with the same held outcome on at least 18 of 24 runs (19 of 24 on the working plant). On the block bed at impratio 10 and 2 ms the pads' twist-onset arm stays within 10 % of its impratio-100 value (2.525 and 2.736 mm at 1 and 3 N) while creep rises about tenfold (10.8 µm/s at 1 N now).

**Falsifier.** The 1 mm pads below 20 of 24 on the servo plant (the overview's agreement claim then covers only the working plant); a single-contact model (pt, ptmc or pt4) at 20 of 24 or more on both plants (it can replace the pads in CPU planners at 13-20 µs per step); 2 mm pads below 18 of 24 on either plant (not a substitute); an onset arm more than 10 % shorter at impratio 10 (the trainer's numerics weaken the friction torque the policies learn on).

**Decision informed.** Which fingertip model the CPU planners and the RL trainer may use without losing agreement with Drake on the plant the policies and planners use; whether the overview's hand-level agreement claim extends to the servo plant; the condim-4 coefficient carried into U4 and U5; the default-tip numbers at the trainer's numerics for the overview.

**Protocol.** Code: new scripts/pad_usability_backends.py (about 150 lines) imports reorient_backends (RB) and runs its loop with these changes: the requested model reaches build_scene (RB.main passes 'pads' if model == 'pads' else 'pt', so a row labelled padsT is point contact); model keys padsT (pads touching the tool only), pads2 (spacing 2 mm, radius 1.5 mm, tool-only, as the RL scenes), pt4 (pt with condim 4, priority 1 and friction '1 <spin> 0.0001' on {f}_tipgeom; spin 2.8 mm on the servo plant and 3.0 mm on the working plant, the pads' onset arm 2.527 F^0.070 mm at each plant's per-finger grip) and ptmc (pt with <flag multiccd='enable'/>); numerics 'rl' (2 ms, implicitfast, elliptic, Newton, 10 iterations, 20 line-search iterations, tolerance 1e-8) used with --ir 10; --dt in ms (NUMERICS['bed']['timestep'] for MuJoCo, RB.DT before DrakeBench is built), entered in the case_id and scene name. Rows fsynced and resumable through RB.done. PY=logs/20261001-hom_contact/venv/bin/python (MuJoCo 3.6, Drake 1.57). Measure one MuJoCo and one Drake rollout with /usr/bin/time -v, then up to three single-core processes, each ~/.claude/bin/resguard.sh run --mem 1G --cpu 100 -- taskset -c <core> $PY ...
(a) Servo plant, seed 0 first as a gate: $PY scripts/pad_usability_backends.py run --hands D1 D2 D3 D4 D5 D6 D7 D8 --tips tpu2.7 --models pads padsT pads2 pt ptmc pt4 --plants kp0.72_kv0_fr0.18_fl0_dp0.0144 --mu 1.0 --ir 100 --seeds 0 1 2 3 4 --out docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/agree/servo_mujoco.jsonl. If the pads keep the tool on fewer than 6 of 8 hands at seed 0, the score counts held agreement and compares turns only where both simulators keep the tool.
(b) Trainer numerics: the same with --models pads pt pt4 --numerics rl --ir 10 --out docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/agree/servo_mujoco_rl.jsonl.
(c) Drake: $PY scripts/pad_usability_backends.py run --sim drake --hands D1 D2 D3 D4 D5 D6 D7 D8 --tips tpu2.7 --plants kp0.72_kv0_fr0.18_fl0_dp0.0144 --mu 1.0 --seeds 0 1 2 --out docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/agree/servo_drake.jsonl.
(d) Drake step check: --sim drake --dt 0.5 --hands D2 D7 --plants kp0.72_kv0_fr0.18_fl0_dp0.0144 kp4_kv0_fr1_fl0_dp0.08 --seeds 0 1 2 --out docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/agree/drake_dt05.jsonl; Drake at 1 ms counts as converged where the end turn moves less than 1 deg.
(e) Working plant, new models only: --models padsT pads2 ptmc pt4 --plants kp4_kv0_fr1_fl0_dp0.08 --mu 1.0 --ir 100 --seeds 0 1 2 3 4 --out docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/agree/work_mujoco.jsonl; the pads, point-contact and Drake rows are docs/experiments/20261006-hom_turn3/plans_mujoco.jsonl, plans_drake.jsonl and docs/experiments/20261006-simulator_agreement/plans_mujoco_pt.jsonl.
(f) HOM closed loop: $PY scripts/hom_turn3.py run --hands D1 D2 D3 D4 D5 D6 D7 D8 --sims mujoco drake --seeds 0 1 2 --plant kp0.72_kv0_fr0.18_fl0_dp0.0144 --mu 1.0 --set kp_model=0.72 --out docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/agree/hom_servo.jsonl (hom_turn3 appends without a resume check, so it runs once).
(g) Block bed at the trainer's numerics: new scripts/pad_usability_bed.py registers mj_tpu2p7_pads1_ir10 ('mj:spheres:s1:rs0.75:ir10:tr0.02:tpu2.7') and mj_tpu2p7_pads2 ('mj:spheres:s2:rs1.5:ir100:tr0.02:tpu2.7') in contact_bed_common.MODELS and contact_bed_pull.MODELS as tpu_tip_rig.bed does for its own models, then calls tpu_tip_rig.bed(models, ['T1', 'T2', 'T5'], [1.0, 3.0], [1.0, 2.0], 'docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/bed'); the impratio-100 pads, multiccd and Drake rows at 1 and 5 ms are docs/experiments/20261006-fingertip_backends/tip_T1, tip_T2 and tip_T5.jsonl.
Score: scripts/simulator_agreement_figure.py's rule (TOL_DEG 3: the model keeps the tool at the end and its end turn is within 3 deg of Drake's) over the 24 Drake placements; HOM within 5 deg with the same held outcome. Checked against the repository: reorient_backends.py has no --dt, no 'rl' numerics and no pt4, ptmc or pads2 keys; its pads radius stays 0.75 mm at any spacing (build_scene passes only s); hom_turn3.py's controller assumes kp_model 4.0 unless --set kp_model=0.72 is given.

**Samples and statistics.** 24 Drake-paired placements per plant and model (D1-D8 x seeds 0-2); seeds 3-4 add 16 placements for comparisons among MuJoCo models and the held count of 40. Per model and plant: within-3 deg count with a Clopper-Pearson 95 % interval; exact McNemar test against the 1 mm pads on the within-3 deg indicator; median |turn difference| with a bootstrap 95 % interval (10,000 resamples); grip ratio F_hold over Drake's; median µs per step from the rows. HOM: 24 pairs, within-5 deg count with the same held outcome, held flips. Bed: one deterministic run per case, relative change against the impratio-100 rows.

**Compute.** CPU only: about 360 MuJoCo replays at 0.2-3 s, 36 Drake rollouts at 25-60 s, 48 HOM runs (Drake 6-8 s each), 12 bed cases at 2-15 s; about 35 min on three single-core processes, RSS under 1 GB each (measured first). Films 15 min. While the owner's planner jobs run, these three processes plus a GPU job stay within 4 cores.

**Depends on.** Nothing from U1's runs. Its wrapper and model keys are reused by U3, and its 1 ms servo-plant rows are U3's reference.

**Films.** Plan replays drawn with reorient_backends_films.film_drake and the wrapper's version of film_mujoco (same camera and labels, scenes built by the wrapper), tiled 3 x 2 with tile_stream: Drake | 1 mm pads | 2 mm pads | condim 4 | point contact | multiccd, for D7 seed 0 on the servo plant and for the servo-plant placement with the widest spread across models; 480x360 panels into docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/agree/media/; poster frames checked at displayed size.

### U3. Largest stable and accurate physics step of the pads and point contact on the hand (order 3, about 0.6 h)

**Hypothesis.** With MuJoCo's refsafe clamp on (time constant raised to 2Δt), the 1 mm pads end the D1-D8 open-loop turn within 3 deg of their own 1 ms result, keeping the tool, on at least 36 of 40 placements at 2, 4 and 5 ms on both plants, and lose agreement at 10 ms, where the clamp makes each sphere four times softer (t_c from 10 to 20 ms); point contact on the TPU block (t_c 20 ms) keeps at least 36 of 40 to 10 ms. With the clamp off, the step at which the pads' one-step map at the held grip turns unstable is set by the friction rows that carry the tool's weight, at the friction-row bound t_c(d0 + (1 − d0)Λ̂/(κλ_t)), about d0·t_c = 8.1 ms for d0 0.81 and t_c 10 ms, with a tool-tangential or rotational unstable mode, as in bed task 7, whose stored friction-row bound brackets 18 of 21 pad pairs and 3 of 3 point-contact pairs. The normal-row bound 2t_c(√(1+q) − 1), q = d0 + (1 − d0)/n (7.0-7.1 ms for 7-30 touching spheres per finger), describes an unloaded pad on a plate.

**Falsifier.** Pads below 36 of 40 at 4 ms on either plant, or any ejection or NaN at 4 ms or less (planner and RL steps stay at 1 and 2 ms); point contact below 36 of 40 at 5 ms while the pads reach 36 (the hand-off ledger's prediction, which makes the pads the model for coarse steps); the clamp-off threshold within 10 % of the normal-row bound with a pad-normal unstable mode (step 9 then states the normal-row bound for the hand); a threshold below both contact bounds with a finger-joint mode (the servo stiffness, explicit in implicitfast, sets the step whatever the contact model).

**Decision informed.** The planner step of U4's 5 ms configurations and the RL evaluation step of U5 (4 ms); which bound the overview's step 9 and deck 23 state for the hand and the pinch; whether impratio, d0 or t_c is the lever for a longer step.

**Protocol.** (a) Spectral thresholds. New scripts/pad_usability_step.py (about 120 lines), a port of d23_step_threshold (/home/humanoid/wxie_workspace/contact/tutorial/09_fingertip_contact.py line 1401; it sits inside a marimo cell and cannot be imported): refsafe off (m.opt.disableflags |= mjDSBL_REFSAFE), 0.2 s at a 0.25 ms step to reach rest, then bisection of the step h in [0.5, 30] ms on the spectral radius of mujoco.mjd_transitionFD(m, d, 1e-9, 1, A, None, None, None) crossing 1 + 1e-3 (a held tool's slow creep sits at radius 1), to 0.01 ms. Per threshold: touching spheres per finger n, d0, Λ̂, the three predictions (2t_c(√(1+q) − 1), q·t_c, and the friction-row bound from λ_t over the loaded tool-pad friction rows, as contact_bed_stability.lam_max computes it on the bed), the unstable eigenvector's share in pad-normal, tool-tangential, tool-rotational and finger-joint coordinates, and 2 s rollouts at 0.95 and 1.05 times the threshold. States: the grip of cylinder:16:0.625 and cylinder:25:0.125 at 0.8 s (servo plant, hand_object_scale_mpc.setup); D6's plan replay at the end of its turn on both plants (U2's wrapper scenes); the servo-plant RL held states exported by U1 (logs/20261014-pad_usability/state_servo_pads1 and state_servo_mesh, world 0, the trainer's 2 ms numerics); the bed rig's 21 pad pairs and 3 point-contact pairs (contact_bed_stability.build(model, dt_ms, tc, d0, refsafe=False) over D0S x TCS_MS and POINT). Models on the scale and bench states: pads, pads2, pt, pt4. Command: ~/.claude/bin/resguard.sh run --mem 1G --cpu 100 -- taskset -c 19 logs/20261001-hom_contact/venv/bin/python scripts/pad_usability_step.py --states scale:cylinder:16:0.625 scale:cylinder:25:0.125 bench:D6:servo bench:D6:work rl:servo_pads1 rl:servo_mesh bed --models pads pads2 pt pt4 --out docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/step/threshold.jsonl.
(b) Step sweep with the clamp on: $PY scripts/pad_usability_backends.py run --hands D1 D2 D3 D4 D5 D6 D7 D8 --tips tpu2.7 --models pads pads2 pt pt4 --plants kp0.72_kv0_fr0.18_fl0_dp0.0144 kp4_kv0_fr1_fl0_dp0.08 --mu 1.0 --ir 100 --seeds 0 1 2 3 4 --dt 2 4 5 10 --out docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/step/plans_dt.jsonl, two processes split by --plants, each resguard run --mem 1G --cpu 100 -- taskset -c <core>. The 1 ms rows are U2's and the 2026-10-06 files'. Steps 2, 4, 5 and 10 ms divide the 20 ms trace period of run_mujoco. Score per model, plant and step: within 3 deg of the same model at 1 ms with both keeping the tool (of 40), within 3 deg of Drake (of 24), held (of 40), ejections, NaN, CPU ms per simulated second.

**Samples and statistics.** Spectral: 6 hand states (the two RL states with their own model, the others x 4 models) plus 24 bed pairs, bisection to 0.01 ms, two confirming rollouts per threshold; a bound counts as met within 10 %. Sweep: 4 models x 4 steps x 2 plants x 40 placements = 1,280 replays; usable step = the largest with at least 36 of 40 within 3 deg of 1 ms and no ejection, reported with the count's exact binomial 90 % interval.

**Compute.** CPU only: spectral about 15 min on one core (one transition matrix of the 15-DoF hand costs about 60 steps); sweep about 20 min on two single-core processes (pads 0.5-2 s per replay, falling with the step; point contact 0.1-0.4 s); RSS under 1 GB each; films 5 min.

**Depends on.** U2 (1 ms servo-plant rows, the wrapper and its model keys); U1 (the exported RL held states for the rl: states; without them those two states are skipped).

**Films.** D7 seed 0 on the servo plant at 1, 4 and 10 ms for the 1 mm pads and point contact (the wrapper's film_mujoco at each step), tiled 3 x 2 with tile_stream into docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/step/media/.

### U5. Fingertip model in training and in evaluation of the servo-plant reorientation policies (order 4, about 1.25 h)

**Hypothesis.** The pads' margin under a weak servo belongs to the pad plant, not to training on pads. Margin now: at kp 0.36 N m/rad the pad finals keep 128 and 128 of 128 closed-loop rollouts on their own plant against 94 and 99 for the TPU-mesh finals, and replays of their own actions at a 0.12 N m torque limit keep 64 and 64 of 64 against 0 and 11. Prediction: on the 1 mm pad plant the mesh finals keep at least 120 of 128 at kp 0.36 and their replays at least 50 of 64 at 0.12 N m; the pad finals on the mesh plant fall to within 10 of 128 of the mesh finals there. The 2 mm pad plant and condim 4 on the TPU mesh (μ_t 2.9 mm) give the 1 mm pad plant's held count within 5 of 128 and held cosine within 0.03 in at least 14 of 16 closed-loop policy-condition pairs. A 4 ms physics step (decimation 5) and impratio 100 (a tenth of the trainer's creep) change no final's held count by more than 3 of 128 or held cosine by more than 0.03.

**Falsifier.** Both pad seeds keep at least 120 of 128 on the mesh plant at kp 0.36 (or at least 50 of 64 replays at 0.12 N m) while both mesh seeds stay at or below 105 of 128 (20 of 64) on the pad plant: a policy effect, so training needs the pads or a cheaper model with torsion. A cheaper plant outside 5 of 128 or 0.03 in more than 2 of its 16 pairs. Impratio 100 or a 4 ms step moving held cosine by more than 0.05, or held count by 5 or more of 128, for both seeds of an arm (the policies rely on the trainer's creep or step).

**Decision informed.** The RL trainer's contact scene: train on the TPU mesh (5.50 against 8.07 s per PPO iteration, 3.3 against 12.5 GB of GPU memory) or on 2 mm pads or condim 4, and score on the 1 mm pads; whether training may step at 4 ms and whether the trainer's impratio of 10 stays. A passing cheaper scene sends two 40 M-step seeds to the owner's training queue (about 1.3 h of GPU each); a policy effect keeps the pads in training. Bench item 6 runs only if the plant effect is found.

**Protocol.** 1. Scenes: U1 step 2 (servo-plant 2 mm pad and condim-4 runs).
2. Code: scripts/pad_usability_rl.py (about 120 lines beyond U1's scenes subcommand) imports rl_contact_eval (E) and contact_feedback_replay (R), adds arms tpu27pads2 and tpu27meshc4 to E.STUDIES['20261011']['arms'] and calls E.use_study('20261011'). Subcommand cross: per plant scene (tpu27mesh, tpu27pads1, tpu27pads2, tpu27meshc4) one E.Evaluator(E.ARMS[scene], ck, n=128, noise=(2.0, 5.0)) for closed loop and one E.Evaluator(E.ARMS[scene], ck, n=64) for replays of the saved own-scene action sequences (docs/experiments/20261013-contact_feedback/acts/<tag>.npz through R.replay); for each final (20261011-d6_servo_tpu27mesh_40M_s0 and _s1, 20261011-d6_servo_tpu27pads1_40M_s0 and _s1; checkpoints from E.final_ckpt) and each condition nominal, kp=0.36, fr=0.12 and friction=0.5 (Evaluator.perturb), one closed-loop and one replay row (E.metrics) to docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/rl/cross.jsonl, fsynced, keyed by (tag, scene, mode, condition). Subcommand numerics: each final on its own scene, closed loop at n = 128 with the same noise and mech=True (hold-phase slide), under E.Evaluator(..., dt=0.004) and under impratio 100 written in place into ev.u.sim.wp_model.opt.impratio_invsqrt (value 0.1; a per-world Warp array in MuJoCo-Warp 3.6, types.py line 765, read by the captured graphs) and restored afterwards; the write counts only if the hold-phase slide falls about tenfold against nominal, else that arm is reported as not run. Rows docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/rl/numerics.jsonl.
3. Launch when nvidia-smi shows at least 2 GB free beyond the job, no owner GPU job runs and U1's GPU jobs have ended: MUJOCO_GL=egl WARP_CACHE_PATH=$(mktemp -d) ~/.claude/bin/resguard.sh run --mem <1.3 x the RSS of the first Evaluator build, measured with /usr/bin/time -v> --cpu 200 -- taskset -c 18 uv run --extra rl --extra gpu python scripts/pad_usability_rl.py cross; then the same prefix with numerics, then film.
Checked against the repository: rl_contact_eval.py transfer takes --scene but no --perturb, robust perturbs only each final's own scene, and the 20261011 study has no pads2 or meshc4 arm; contact_feedback_replay.py perturbs only own scenes; Evaluator(noise=...) takes (mm, deg); no runtime impratio option exists, hence the in-place write.

**Samples and statistics.** 4 finals (2 seeds per training arm) x 4 plants x 4 conditions x 2 modes = 128 evaluations, plus 4 finals x 2 numerics conditions. Closed loop: 128 rollouts with uniform 2 mm and 5 deg placement noise; replays: the 64 recorded sequences. Held (two fingertips each pressing at least 0.24 N, tool above 60 mm at 5 s) with Wilson 95 % intervals; held cosine (signed cosine of the tool's axis with vertical over held rollouts) with bootstrap 95 % intervals. A plant effect (same policy, pad minus mesh plant) or policy effect (same plant, pad-trained minus mesh-trained) counts only when all four seed-level comparisons share the sign and each exceeds 10 of 128 (5 of 64 for replays); rollout-level Fisher exact p-values are reported beside them; the seed is the unit of replication.

**Compute.** GPU 3-6 GB, one CPU core, host RSS about 4-5 GB (measured on the first build). 10 env builds at about 2 min, 136 evaluations at 6-12 s, films 10 min: about 1.2 h. One GPU job at a time.

**Depends on.** U1 step 2 (the servo-plant 2 mm pad and condim-4 scenes) and the end of U1's GPU jobs. Runs on the GPU while U3 and U4 use the CPU.

**Films.** At kp 0.36, closed loop, the median-final-cosine world of mesh s0 on the mesh plant beside mesh s0 on the 1 mm pad plant, and of pad s0 on the pad plant beside pad s0 on the mesh plant, drawn with Evaluator(watch=True).render_world at 480x360 with contact_feedback_replay.film's label bars and strips, into docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/rl/media/20261014-rl_cross_kp036.mp4; pad s0 at 2 ms beside 4 ms; a strip of each with scripts/policy_filmstrip.py --video <clip> --at 20,58,90,120,160,248 --cols 3.

### U4. Pinned iCEM with cheaper rollout models on the 1 mm pad plant (order 5, about 2 h)

**Hypothesis.** Pinned iCEM (config icem_pin: 64 rollouts of 0.5 s every 20 ms, first knot pinned to the command in force) that rolls out condim 4 on the TPU block (μ_t 2.8 mm), or the 1 mm pads colliding with the cylinder only at the GPU planner's solver settings, turns the cylinder on the 1 mm pad plant (servo plant, real_v1) with a median paired change in held turn of at least -10 deg against the pads planner (median 64.6 deg on seeds 0 and 1) and ends at least 15 of 16 runs with three loaded fingers, at a third or less of the pads planner's replanning time (0.83 s for 64 plans on 6 threads). Point contact without torsion (condim 3) loses more than 10 deg or a finger in at least 2 of 16 runs, because its model lets the cylinder pivot about the pinch axis, which the pad plant brakes. Planning at a 5 ms step changes the median by less than 10 deg. The adopted planner ends at least 7 of 8 runs with three loaded fingers, median within 15 deg of its nominal runs, when the plant's sliding friction falls to 0.5 and 0.7 while the planner keeps 1.

**Falsifier.** The condim-4 planner: median paired change below -10 deg with a cell-cluster bootstrap 95 % lower bound below -15 deg, or fewer than 15 of 16 three-finger endings (the planner needs the pads' distributed torsion). If the condim-3 planner also passes both tests, the turn needs no friction torque in the planner's model and the cheapest model is adopted. The friction arm fails with fewer than 7 of 8 three-finger endings at friction 0.5 (MPC needs friction randomisation in its rollouts before a bench run).

**Decision informed.** The rollout model, step and solver settings of the CPU MPC (pads as now; pads filtered with the GPU solver settings; condim 4 or condim 3 on the block; 1 or 5 ms), with the pads kept as the scoring plant; whether a 34-cell planner landscape at three placements fits on the CPU; whether MPC rollouts need friction randomisation.

**Protocol.** Code: new scripts/pad_usability_planner_model.py (about 150 lines) imports hand_object_scale_mpc (M), hand_object_scale_planners (prm_of, SERVO) and hand_object_scale_sim (S) and edits none of them (the owner's planner study uses them). It wraps M.setup to attach the cell's scene paths to the plant (pads, pt, and pads2 built with reorient_backends.replace_tips(s=0.002, rs=0.0015) as <tag>_pads2__<plant>.xml) and installs a Planner subclass as M.Planner (mpc_turn and film_pair look the name up at call time). The subclass compiles the planner model from the scene of prm['plan_tip'] (pads, pads2, pt, pt4) with sensor_model's per-finger contact sensors and asserts equal nq, nv and FULLPHYSICS state size (32 values); pt4 sets condim 4, priority 1 and friction (1, 0.0028, 1e-4) on the three {f}_tipgeom geoms (priority 1 is required: every cell-scene geom, the cylinder included, has torsional friction 0.005, and MuJoCo takes the larger at equal priority); pad_filter as now; plan_tol, plan_iter and plan_ls set opt.tolerance, iterations and ls_iterations; plan_dt sets opt.timestep with cost_every = round(0.01 / plan_dt), and the subclass returns controls interpolated from the chosen knots at the plant's 1 ms step, so mpc_turn's n_exec stays dt_plan over the plant step. Configurations (icem_pin plus): pads; fs (pad_filter 1, plan_tol 1e-6, plan_iter 20, plan_ls 10, the GPU planner's values); fs5 (fs at plan_dt 0.005); pads2 (only if U1 passes it); pt; pt5 (pt at 5 ms); pt4. Rows: every mpc_turn field plus plan_tip, plan_dt, plant_set, hand_model; fsynced, resumable by (config, cell, seed, variant), docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/planner/turn.jsonl; the pads planner's seeds 0 and 1 are the 8 icem_pin rows of docs/experiments/20261013-scale_turn_controllers/planners/turn.jsonl.
1. Quote ~/.claude/bin/resguard.sh status; RSS: /usr/bin/time -v .venv/bin/python scripts/pad_usability_planner_model.py run --configs pt4 --cells cylinder:25:0.125 --seeds 0 --nthread 3 --out logs/20261014-pad_usability/planner_probe.jsonl.
2. Reproduction: the pads configuration on cylinder:25:0.125 seed 0 must end within 0.5 deg of the existing row (91.19 deg).
3. While pgrep -f '[h]and_object_scale_planners' finds the owner's jobs: ~/.claude/bin/resguard.sh run --mem <1.3 x RSS> --cpu 400 -- taskset -c 17-19 .venv/bin/python scripts/pad_usability_planner_model.py run --configs pt pt4 pt5 fs5 --cells cylinder:25:0.125 cylinder:16:0.625 cylinder:10:0.75 cylinder:16:-0.375 --seeds 0 1 2 3 --nthread 2 --out docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/planner/turn.jsonl (fs5 only if U3 keeps the pads at 5 ms).
4. After they end: --configs pads fs [pads2] --seeds 0 1 2 3 --nthread 6 as two shards (--shard 0/2 under taskset -c 6-12, --shard 1/2 under taskset -c 13-19, --cpu 700 each).
5. Gated on steps 3-4: the cheapest passing configuration with SCALE_HAND=cad_v1 on the four cells, seeds 0 and 1, --out docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/planner/turn_cad.jsonl, paired with the icem_pin rows of docs/experiments/20261014-scale_turn_cad_v1/planners/turn.jsonl; and the same configuration (the pads if none passes) on seeds 0 and 1 with --variant mu0.5 --plant-set mu=0.5 and --variant mu0.7 --plant-set mu=0.7 (the plant's sliding friction; the planner's model compiles from the XML at 1.0, since icem_pin's contact cost recompiles it).
Checked against the repository: hand_object_scale_planners.py has no icem_pin_pt, icem_pin_fs or plan_tip configurations, and STRING_KEYS admits only method and device; Planner reads pad_filter but has no model, step or solver options; mpc_turn indexes the planner's control rows at the plant's step, so a 5 ms planner needs the interpolation above; the plant friction flag is --plant-set mu=<v>.

**Samples and statistics.** Unit: a (cell, seed) pair; a seed sets the cylinder's placement jitter and the planner's random stream, and runs are deterministic, so arms are paired. real_v1: 16 pairs per arm (4 cells x seeds 0-3); CAD hand and each friction variant: 8 pairs. Primary outcome: held turn (end turn if the cylinder is less than 20 mm below its post-grip height with two fingers each pressing at least its weight, else 0) minus the pads planner's on the same pair; median with a cell-cluster bootstrap 95 % interval (10,000 resamples) and an exact Wilcoxon signed-rank test. Secondary: three-finger endings (three_frac at least 0.99; exact McNemar test against the pads planner), contact breaks, cmd_rate_rms, omega_jitter, peak rate, planner seconds per replanning (plan_s / 275). Cell effects (34-91 deg for the pads planner) exceed seed effects (0.1-16 deg), so the test pairs within cells.

**Compute.** CPU only, RSS about 1.5 GB per process (measured first). At 6 threads: pads 207 s per run (8 runs plus the reproduction, 16 min as two shards), fs about 80-120 s (16 runs, 13-16 min as two shards), pt and pt4 about 30 s (32 runs, 16 min), pt5 and fs5 under 30 s (32 runs, 10 min); at 2-3 threads the cheap arms take two to three times longer. CAD and friction arms about 25 min; films 15 min. At most 4 of our cores while the owner's planner jobs run, about 14 afterwards.

**Depends on.** U1 (replanning time per configuration; pads2 joins only if its time passes) and U3 (5 ms configurations run only if the pads and point contact keep at least 36 of 40 open-loop placements at 4-5 ms). The heavy arms wait for the owner's planner jobs to end.

**Films.** Through M.film_pair with the subclass installed: the pad plant under the pads, condim-4 and condim-3 planners (panel titles 'Pads planner', 'Condim-4 planner', 'Point-contact planner'), cells cylinder:25:0.125 and cylinder:16:-0.375, seed 0, 640x480 panels three across (tile_stream), pads touching the cylinder drawn red, one films.jsonl row per clip: MUJOCO_GL=egl .venv/bin/python scripts/pad_usability_planner_model.py film --configs pads pt4 pt --cells cylinder:25:0.125 cylinder:16:-0.375 --seed 0 --cols 3 --size 640x480 --out-dir docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/planner/media; a friction-0.5 clip of the adopted planner if that arm runs.

## Bench measurements for the owner

- **Pull test for the friction coefficient of the printed TPU tip on the bench tool.** Two printed 95A 20 % gyroid tips pinch the bench cylinder at 0.5, 1 and 2 N per tip (weights or a spring scale); pull along the axis until it slips, 5 repeats per load, static and kinetic slip noted; μ = F/(2N); fit with scripts/tpu_tip_calibrate.py fit --tip w95_20 --pull N:F. About 30 min. Decides: The scenes' μ (1.0 now) and the RL friction randomisation range (x0.55-1.15): a μ below 0.55 lies outside it and calls for retraining with a wider range; with U4's friction arm, whether MPC rollouts need randomised friction at the measured value. Expected: Open-loop replays keep 292 of 384 rollouts at friction x0.5 against 376 of 384 closed loop; μ 0.6 instead of 1.0 lowers the simulated bench turn by about 10 deg; the bench joint readbacks cannot separate μ 0.6 from 1.0.

- **Servo push test of fingertip stiffness and overload trip.** The bench plan's push test (docs/experiments/20261011-bench_plan/plan.json, 39 min), station stopped while the bus is read: index and thumb fingertips pushed along the contact normal in 0.25 N steps to the overload trip, 3 series each; stiffness by least squares on force against displacement, and the trip force. Decides: Which plant every simulation conclusion rests on (servo-limited 0.72 N m/rad and 0.18 N m, or the 1 N m working plant). At 0.9 N/mm or more the gyroid tip (0.063 mm/N) reaches 5 % of the finger's compliance and the tip model re-enters the grip force, so U2-U5 must be rechecked on the measured plant. Expected: Predicted 0.29 N/mm with a 4.1 N trip (servo plant) against 1.59 N/mm and 22.6 N (working plant), 5.5 times apart; pad- against mesh-trained policy replays keep 85 of 88 against 3 of 132 on the working plant and 78 of 88 against 74 of 88 on the servo plant.

- **Twist-onset torque of the printed tip at 1 and 3 N per tip.** Same fixture as the pull test; a hanging mass on a 50 mm lever in 1 g steps (0.49 mN m) until the tool spins, 5 repeats at 1 and 3 N per tip; arm = T/(2μN) with μ from the pull test at the same load; scripts/tpu_tip_calibrate.py fit --twist N:T. About 45 min. Decides: Whether the pads' friction arm on the block needs an edge weighting from the finite-element load share, and the condim-4 coefficient μ_t that U4 and U5 may adopt (2.8-2.9 mm now, taken from the pads). Expected: Finite elements 2.93 and 3.12 mm, pads 2.53 and 2.74 mm, Drake 2.50 and 2.62 mm at 1 and 3 N; at 3 N the gap between finite elements and pads is 2.3 mN m, 4.6 g on the lever; an arm of 3.0 mm or more at 3 N means the pads under-predict torsion by 9 % or more.

- **Indentation of the printed tip at 0.5, 1 and 3 N.** A 12.5 mm rod pressed across the palmar face; approach read from first touch with a dial indicator of 2 µm resolution, 3 repeats per load; fit with scripts/tpu_tip_calibrate.py fit --indent. About 20 min. Decides: The pad modulus (scenes use 10 MPa; finite-element fits of the planned prints give 6.6-16.5 MPa) and the magnet tip's force calibration. Lowest control priority: the block's arm changes 10 % over 5-20 MPa and the contact is 79-278 times stiffer than the servo. Expected: Finite elements predict 21.5, 41.4 and 114.9 µm for the magnet tip (30 MPa solid; the G-code model gives 63 µm at 1 N) with a force exponent of 1.07 against the pads' 1.5.

- **In-hand pinch brake on the D6 hand, tracked by the tag.** Thumb and index pinch the bench cylinder with its centre of mass 15 mm off the pinch line; the commanded squeeze stepped down 0.5 deg per second, tag at 30 Hz recorded from before the grip, 10 repeats, after scripts/real_v1_trajectory_clearance.py passes the brake plan (chord and _traj.csv separately). Prediction rows from scripts/hom_hand_brake.py on the servo plant with block pads and point contact, computed before the session. About 45 min. Decides: Whether the printed tip transmits the friction torque that separates the pads from point contact, in the one maneuver whose model differences exceed the bench scatter; whether braked swings may be planned on the pads. Expected: Point contact releases the tool into a free swing at the ramp's start in MuJoCo and Drake; on the block the pads pass 45 deg at 0.34 N per pad and Drake at 0.50 N, both ending at 69-78 deg; the tag resolves 0.05-0.09 deg.

- **Torque-limited open-loop replays of TPU-mesh and pad policy targets on the D6 hand.** Only if U5 finds a plant effect. Median-world finger targets of mesh s1 and pads s1 exported from logs/20261011-servo_plant_policies/replay/<tag>/rec.npz into the real_v1 plan format (new scripts/rl_replay_to_plan.py, about 40 lines), clearance-gated, copied to the CB1 as individual files; 10 tracked trials per policy at the 0.18 N m limit and 10 at the register equivalent of 0.12 N m, interleaved, servo_load logged; held = tool above 60 mm with two fingertips loaded at the end; Fisher exact test between limits per policy. About 2.5 h. Decides: Which simulated plant scores the RL policies (1 mm pads or TPU mesh), and so whether U5's rule of training on a cheap tip and scoring on the pads carries to the hand. Expected: Simulated replays of mesh s1 at 0.12 N m keep 11 of 64 on the mesh plant against at least 50 of 64 on the pad plant if U5's plant effect holds; 3 or fewer of 10 on the bench means the printed tip acts like point contact at these grips.

- **Bench bookkeeping for comparing future runs with simulation.** Weigh the vaned tool (1 min); record the palm plate height (25 mm above the mounting plane in the built hand) and the servo bank's envelope; start the tracker before the grip so the pose on the post is logged; servo_load on every run; present-position reads interleaved with the 50 Hz command stream; copy the CB1 run logs to a dated archive folder after each session; commit logs/tracker CSVs to LFS. Decides: Whether later bench runs can be replayed in simulation at all: the 177 tracked runs of 2026-08-31 to 09-02 have no joint data during the turn and no pre-grip pose, and their within-hand scatter (12.9 deg) exceeds every contact-model difference on 6 of 8 hands. Expected: No new effect; removes five confounds of the earlier runs (plate height, servo bank, vane mass, grip duration, tool pose on the post).

## Documentation plan

Result page. New builder scripts/pad_usability_page.py with template scripts/pad_usability_page.template.html, copied from scripts/scale_turn_cad_v1_page.py (the newest builder: retro_style.apply, texsvg for every equation, films inlined as data URIs). Output docs/experiments/20261014-pad_usability/20261014-sphere_pad_cost_and_control.html, title "Cost and control outcomes of the sphere-pad fingertip on the real_v1 hand". Order: a lede of about 100 words naming the adopted planner model and its replanning time, the RL scene decision and the usable physics step, with numbers; Terms and metrics (held, held turn, three-finger ending, contact break, within 3 deg of Drake, replanning time, µs per step and per world-step, PPO iteration time, spectral radius, d0, t_c, q, λ_t, μ_t, impratio, creep); one section per experiment U1-U5 with its table, figure and film; Limitations stated once; Future work naming the bench items with their measurements and falsifiers and the owner-queue items (two 40 M-step seeds on the adopted scene; MPC on a Drake plant). Rows stay in the folder's subfolders (cost/, agree/, step/, planner/, rl/, bed/). Run scripts/page_figure_check.py; add a row to docs/experiments/INDEX.md (2026-10-14, Fingertip contact model, local file, artifact URL after publishing, artifact_url.txt in the folder).

Overview docs/overviews/contact_model.html (builder scripts/contact_overview_page.py, prose scripts/contact_overview_text.py, template scripts/contact_overview_page.template.html; claimed by this job; rebuild with .venv/bin/python). (1) Lede reframed on usability (owner 2026-10-14): one fingertip contact that runs unmodified in CPU MuJoCo for planners and in MuJoCo-Warp for RL, with hand numbers: 105.6 µs per step on the D1-D8 replay against 12.9 µs for point contact and 2,082 µs for Drake; U1's cylinder-only and 2 mm figures; 1.59-1.75 µs per world-step against Newton hydroelastic's 4.25-4.34 µs in one MuJoCo-Warp 3.14 build (same_state_timing.jsonl), with the pads' 1.7-2.2 times Newton's GPU memory; PPO 1.47-1.82 times the mesh and 11.5-12.5 GB; then U4's and U5's control results. "Reproduces the hydroelastic pressure law" replaces "reproduces the friction torque and contact area of a soft fingertip". (2) A "Related contact models" paragraph: Simbody/OpenSim ElasticFoundationForce (2011); Masterjohn et al., RA-L 2022 (Drake turns each pressure-field polygon into a point contact of stiffness g·A with its own Coulomb cone); Röstel et al. 2026 (Newton passes SDF triangles to MuJoCo-Warp the same way); CSLC (Nechyporenko et al. 2026); Gonthier's volumetric model; Gazebo's load-scheduled torsion; MuJoCo's documented slow slippage. The parts specific to this work: the load-time calibration d0 = 1 - 1/(t_c^2 K Λ̂), the creep law with cone saturation, the step bound, one model in three simulators, and Newton's missing effective-mass factor (a note). Newton's settings are stated once (friction rows of 3.9 ms on the bed and about 5 ms on the hand, contact reduction without moment matching) in place of a "better than Newton" framing. (3) The printed TPU block becomes the headline tip from existing rows (docs/experiments/20261006-fingertip_backends/tip_T1, T2, T5.jsonl): onset arm 2.525 and 2.736 mm against Drake's 2.496 and 2.618 mm at 1 and 3 N; sliding arm 4.55 against 3.59 mm at 1 N; the braked tool passes 45 deg at 0.338 against 0.500 N; creep 10.8 against 1.19 µm/s; 57 against 531 µs per step; plus U2's impratio-10 rows. The 10.55 mm sphere stays as the analytic check. (4) One paragraph and one film of U1-U5 with a link to the new page; the RL paragraph leads with the servo plant. (5) The cost table gains hand rows. RELATED gains 20261006-fingertip_backends, 20261011-servo_plant_policies, 20261013-scale_turn_controllers, 20261014-scale_turn_cad_v1, 20261013-cad_v1_finger and the new page.

Deck-23 intuitions to add (typeset with texsvg). Step 1: the hinge balance 2μF r̄(F) >= mgd cos φ (mgd = 3.61 mN m; capacity 1.95 mN m at 1 N per pad; horizontal from 1.64 N per pad, about 0.72 N on the block; point contact keeps the tool only hanging; quasi-static 85.9 deg at 0.2 N for the Brake paragraph), and the arm as a force-weighted mean distance, r̄/a = 1/2, 8/15, 3π/16, 2/3 and 1 for cone, paraboloid, Hertz, uniform and rim loading from the ring integrals. Step 2: the sagitta δ0 - ρ^2/2R, a^2 = 2Rδ0 and the substitution to F = πk_wRδ0^2 and r̄ = (8/15)(4R/πk_w)^(1/4) F^(1/4); Drake's 10 MPa as the field parameter k_wR (Hertz with a 10 MPa material gives 0.51 mm against 1.035 mm at 1 N). Step 3: the torque form of the ratio test (9.39 against 10.90 from 0.5 to 3 N; r̄ = T/F_slip removes μ), its third outcome (exponent 0.15 gives 1.31) and its restriction to spherical tips (the block's FE arm grows 1.02-1.11 times). Step 5: the inset R - r_s (contact density (R/(R - r_s))^2, arm factor ((R - r_s)/R)^(1/4) = 0.982) and the placement spread of coarse pads. Step 6: the row identity R f = a_ref - a1, which removes the coupling at rest and gives k_s = 1/((1 - d0)Λ̂t_c^2); the rank-one A of one pad (13 x 90.74 = 1,180 kg^-1); the transient r'' + (2/t_c)r' + r/t_c^2 = (1 - d0)a0, which returns as (1 + t/t_c)e^(-t/t_c) and is within 5 % after 4.74 t_c (47 ms). Steps 6-7: the units that bring the masses in (k in s^-2, R in kg^-1), the softest spring 1/(Λ̂t_c^2) and the bound t_c > (k_sΛ̂)^(-1/2) (3.66 ms bed, 5.1 ms thumb), the tool-mass factor (w_p + 1/m0)/(w_p + 1/(s m0)). Step 8: the friction row as a damper c = 2κk_s t_c (1,641.9 N s/m per sphere, free of d0 and Λ̂), cone saturation F_t = Σ min(cv, μf_n,i) giving MuJoCo's 22.7, 30.8 and 53.3 µm/s, and why a finer pad creeps as fast. Step 9: the semi-implicit step matrix with hc < 2 and h^2ω^2 + 2hc < 4, the one-row bound 0.828 t_c and the n-row bound 2t_c(√(1+q) - 1) (7.394 against 8.761 ms for the 13-sphere plate pad), followed by U3's result on which rows set the step in the pinch and on the hand. RL paragraph (from the control reader): servo 250 N/m in series with a 34.7 kN/m contact gives 248 N/m, so the tip model drops out of the grip; the block brake μΣF_f r̄(F_f) with r̄ = 2.527 F^0.070 mm (40-50 mN m at the servo plant's 13-18 N grips, 130-170 mN m at 44-55 N).

Consistency fixes (claims reader). One creep factor per tip and step (sphere 155 times at 1 ms and impratio 100, 164/155/156 over 0.5/1/3 N; 34 times at 5 ms; 9 times on the block), deleting 100 times (Table 1, refs_text) and 177 times (step 8 pools point contact). The task-6 sentence with clean values (30.76, 3.08, 1.03, 0.31 µm/s at impratio 100, 1000, 3000, 10,000; noslip 3 or 10 gives -1.56 µm/s). Figure 7's caption: its twist and brake columns are the sphere bed. Steps 5, 6 and 8 name the body of each number (bed: 126 spheres on a 35 deg cap, 820.96 N/m, Λ̂ 90.74 kg^-1, d0 0.866; chain thumb: 205 spheres on a 45 deg cap, 817 N/m, 47.2 kg^-1, d0 0.741). The 2 mm pad's resolution as a placement range (-46 to +4 %). Eq. (14) replaced as above. "Relaxes in 2t_c" removed from step 6 and the glossary. body_invweight0 "computed once at qpos0". Point contact's torque about the normal reported as zero, with the onset protocol's 0.07-0.14 mm offset footnoted in Table 2. The spinning arm "at 1 rad/s" with its rate dependence (0.888-0.973 mm at 0.1-3 rad/s). Condim 4 reproduces Drake's spinning torque and sits 5-8 % above the law at onset. The Future-work item on creep in the whole task replaced by its 2026-10-06 result (chain at impratio 100-10,000, swing end 90.5-91.6 deg; the deployed turn loses grasps at 10,000) and the untested wield. Newton's one-world cost 0.42 and 0.70 ms. The modulus item rewritten (block arm 2.66 to 2.39 mm from 5 to 20 MPa; FE pad moduli 6.6-16.5 MPa; text drafted in docs/handoff/20261007-tpu_tip_finite_elements.md). The ratio test limited to spherical tips. Header dates 1-14 October 2026; the printed TPU block named as the default tip since 13 October; patch radius 1.6-2.6 mm. RL text: servo plant first (replays 78 of 88 against 74 of 88; tip swap 1,527 against 1,531 of 1,536), and direct pads-mesh closed-loop transfer 60 against 65 % without the skin. RL cost as 1.47-1.82 times per PPO iteration and 11.5-12.5 GB, with the 12 % labelled zero-action env throughput.

Outside this job's claimed files, listed for the owner rather than edited: scripts/servo_plant_policies_page.py lines 62 and 265 compute the pad brake with the sphere's arm (R_ARM_MM 1.00, F^1.25; the block gives 37-50 mN m and F^1.07); scripts/hand_object_scale_page.py line 1052 calls the pads "calibrated against Drake" (they are built from E/h); CLAUDE.md line 24 and memory feedback_default_fingertip_1mm_pads.md give 1.7x per RL iteration (1.47x servo plant, 1.74x working plant, 11.5-12.5 GB against 2.2-3.3 GB).

Deck 23. The slide reminder exists (commit 623ec2ff: the overview builder prints the commits since the deck last took its numbers; CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md point to it). After the rebuild run .venv/bin/python scripts/contact_overview_page.py --deck-check, read the page at its displayed size, and write /home/humanoid/wxie_workspace/contact/docs/handoff/20261014-deck23-sync.md under that repository's AGENTS.md process, listing the slides to change: creep-live notes (pooled 16.8 to 1.0 and -0.54 µm/s), results-used-later (ratio test as the printed tip's bench test), models-table (150 times creep), pad-step-bound and step-bound-data (U3's result), against-drake (block rows), the twist test described as a k_w measurement (a 10 % arm error is a 40 % k_w error) and the 19-of-21 bracketing attributed to rail damping. Set DECK_CHECKED only if nothing changes. Ledger: steps 2, 3 and 4 of docs/handoff/20261014-contact_model_assessment.md become U3, U1 and U4; U2 and U5 are added with ap step add.

## Experiments considered and dropped

- Newton hydroelastic at its best settings on the hand turn and the bed, with GPU cost in one build (E4, V3): Owner direction 2026-10-14 (hand-off 'Do not', memory feedback_pads_usability_not_newton_physics.md): no accuracy comparisons that try to separate the pads from Newton hydroelastic; same-build GPU costs already exist (MuJoCo-Warp 3.14: pads 1.59-1.75 µs against 4.25-4.34 µs per world-step).
- Contact bed on the TPU block with a converged Drake, three spacings and three cylinder diameters (V4): The overview's default-tip numbers come from the existing tip_T1/T2/T5 rows and U2 adds the trainer's numerics and 2 mm pads; whether the 0.338 against 0.500 N brake gap is Drake's discretization is a reference question bench item 5 answers on the hand.
- Three-pad MPC on a Drake plant (RC4): About 1.5 h of wrapper code and 1.3 h of runs; U2's HOM in Drake and U4's mismatch and friction arms test controller transfer first; it becomes the next step once a cheap planner is adopted and MPC heads to the bench.
- Full contact-parameter sensitivity of MPC and HOM (RC3): Reduced to U4's friction arm; a 250 N/m servo in series with a 20-69 kN/m contact makes pad stiffness move the grip by under 1 %, and creep enters U5 as impratio 100.
- Exposing the trainer's solver iterations and impratio as config fields (RC5): Changes the trainer's env build; U5 tests impratio 100 on the trained policies with a runtime write first.
- Training a 40 M-step policy on a cheaper scene in this session (E2 stage C): One seed cannot separate scenes (held-cosine seed spread 0.09); two seeds go to the owner's training queue if U5 passes a cheaper scene.
- A --sim-timestep trainer option and lowered contact buffers for every scene (E2): The 4 ms step is evaluated with Evaluator(dt=0.004) in U5; env_build's documented training needs (516 contacts, 2,442 rows, 595 matches per world) exceed the zero-action sizes the proposal assumed, so overrides stay a gated step of U1.
- Plan-ranking screen of 176 states x 64 plans (E1 step 2): U4's paired closed-loop runs decide the planner model; rank correlations of single replannings have no control threshold of their own.
- Point-contact GPU planner (E1 option): The GPU planner already uses cylinder-only pads at 3.5 µs per world-step, and at 64 plans its cost is kernel launches, not contact.
- Front-half and census-pruned pad regions: On the GPU the front half costs as much as the full set (2.41 against 2.57 µs per world-step); on the CPU U1's cylinder-only filter and 2 mm pads test the same savings.
- Pads with a 15 ms time constant (E3): Changes the default tip's creep and contact response; the longer clamp-free step it gives (7.5 instead of 5 ms) matters only if U3 shows 5 ms steps failing.
- HOM and logged MPC commands replayed in Drake on the scale-study cells (V2 part 2): Needs SceneGraph collision-filter changes for the post; U2 runs HOM in Drake on the eight bench hands.
- Replaying the 177 tracked bench runs with point contact and Drake: Within-hand scatter (12.9 deg) and per-hand offsets (up to 23 deg) exceed the model differences (at most 4.4 deg on 6 of 8 hands), and the owner ruled out fitting bench runs on 2026-10-06.
- Spin-rate sweep of the sliding arm and the onset-protocol offset (deck reader): Contact mechanics without a control decision; the overview states the 1 rad/s rate and reports point contact's arm as zero.
- MuJoCo 3.14 portability tests, CPU/GPU trace rows and a per-world d0 re-solve: Prerequisites of a software release the owner has not asked for; listed in the overview's Future work.
- CSLC comparison, FE or IPC twist and brake reference, edge and rolling tasks: No CSLC code is available and no hand task uses edges or rolling; items for a standalone paper.
- Newton hydroelastic on Warp's CPU device: Newton's hydroelastic pipeline targets CUDA; the CPU claim is made against Drake and point contact, which U1 and U2 measure.
- Presliding of the printed tip with a 2 µm indicator: Matters only if the magnet tip's touch signal enters a policy; the pads store no presliding and creep instead.
- Bench proposals E5, RC6 and V5 as session experiments: No hardware in this session; they are bench items 1-6.

## Contribution assessment

Yes, the sphere pads are a contribution, sized for a section of the hand paper or a four-page workshop paper. They make MuJoCo's soft contact rows sample a hydroelastic pressure law identically in CPU MuJoCo, MuJoCo-Warp and Newton, and step 9 to 86 times faster than Drake on one CPU core and 3 to 8 times slower than point contact. For control of the hand they matter where friction torque holds the tool; on the servo-limited plant the tip model leaves RL outcomes unchanged at nominal servo strength. A standalone paper needs a measured fingertip and a hardware outcome the pads change; neither exists.

The discretization has precedent. Simbody's ElasticFoundationForce (2011) puts a spring with drifting friction at each mesh face. Since Masterjohn et al. (RA-L 2022) Drake replaces each hydroelastic polygon by a point contact of stiffness pressure gradient times area with its own Coulomb cone, and Newton passes its SDF triangles to MuJoCo-Warp the same way (Röstel et al., 2026). CSLC (Nechyporenko et al., 2026) gives sphere fingertips distributed contact but never tests torsion. The new parts are a mapping that solves each sphere's impedance from MuJoCo's compiled inverse inertia, making every sphere a spring of modulus times area over layer depth for any tool mass (1,199.8 N/m for 6 to 98 g tools), and a creep law with cone saturation that predicts MuJoCo's sliding to four digits. The work also found that Newton's SolverMuJoCo path multiplies hydroelastic stiffness by the effective mass (sink 7.1 to 7.4 times Drake's).

The friction arm is twisting torque over μ times normal force; creep is sliding speed under a held load below slip. On the two-pad bed's 10.55 mm sphere tip the pad's spin-onset arm is 6.2 to 6.5 % below Drake's at 0.5 to 3 N, and its arm spinning at 1 rad/s is within 1.2 % of the integrated law. Creep is 30.8 against Drake's 0.20 µm/s at 1 N and impratio 100, ten times faster at the trainer's impratio of 10 (MuJoCo's friction-to-normal stiffness ratio). On the printed TPU block, the default tip since 13 October, the onset arm is within 5 % of Drake's, but the sliding arm is 18 to 66 % long, the braked tool passes 45° at a pinch 32 % below Drake's, and creep is 9 times Drake's. Drake shares the law and the unmeasured 10 MPa modulus; finite elements of the printed tip put the pads' indentation 20 to 28 % and arm 7 to 23 % off.

In the pinch brake and the pick-brake-insert chain a single point contact lets the tool swing free in Drake and MuJoCo, and the pads, Drake hydroelastic and condim 4 with load-rescheduled torsion complete both (one run each). On the working plant (1 N·m finger limit, 4.4 times the servo stall torque, 44 to 96 N grips), pad policy targets held the tool in 85 of 88 open-loop replays in other simulators against 3 of 132 for point-contact policies. On the servo-limited plant (0.18 N·m) each fingertip is a 250 N/m servo spring, 139 times softer than the contact; replays of pad and mesh policies hold 78 and 74 of 88, and a policy's own actions replayed on another tip hold 1,527 of 1,536 rollouts. Pad training costs 1.47 to 1.82 times the mesh per PPO iteration and 11.5 to 12.5 GB of GPU memory against 2.2 to 3.3 GB.

The pads are closer to Drake than Newton hydroelastic in every configuration tested: median deviation 6.2 % against 19.3 % over nine bed metrics (about 18 % with 10 to 20 ms friction time constants), and 23 against 7 of 24 hand-turn placements within 3° of Drake. That turn ran Newton with a 5 ms friction time constant, which overshoots on the bed, and reduced contacts without moment matching; Newton creeps 3.7 to 7.0 times less than the pads. At 4,096 worlds on the pinch fixture the pads cost 1.08 µs per world-step, Newton hydroelastic 1.85 µs reduced and 4.28 µs unreduced, and the same pads in Newton 1.23 µs. On the hand's RL state they cost 1.6 to 2.4 against 4.3 µs, on a different MuJoCo-Warp build and with 1.7 to 2.2 times Newton's GPU memory; Newton has no CPU timing.

A reviewer would first call the pads Masterjohn's discretization rebuilt in MuJoCo and checked only against Drake, then raise cheaper baselines. Condim 4 completes the brake and chain at 28 % of the pads' physics time and, fitted to Drake, deviates from it by a median 5.7 % against the pads' 6.2 %. On the block, MuJoCo's multi-contact mesh collision (multiccd) brakes the swing to 65.4° against the pads' 69.1° and Drake's 73.3° at a third of the pads' cost, though it slips at 92 % of the Coulomb force and overshoots to 124° at a 5 ms step. Creep and headline numbers from a sphere tip that no scene uses come next.

A section and appendix of the hand paper need the block-tip bed as headline, the corrected step bound and one cost table (two hours of compute). A workshop paper adds Newton at its best settings on the hand turn, condim 4 derived from the law and the multiccd baseline. A journal letter adds bench tests of the printed tip, a comparison with CSLC and a hardware result the pads change. A software release takes about four days.

### Claims that survive as stated

- Load-time calibration: a constant-impedance sphere with d0 = 1 - 1/(t_c^2 K Lambda), Lambda read from the compiled body_invweight0 of tip and tool, is a spring of E*a/h at rest in unmodified MuJoCo: 1,199.8 N/m against the 1,200 N/m target for tools of 6.1, 24.5 and 98.2 g, while pads built for another tool are off by -61 % and +65 % (docs/experiments/20261013-contact_feedback/pad_laws.json; 15 of 15 tests in tests/test_pad_contact_laws.py pass on MuJoCo 3.6).
- On the 10.55 mm sphere tip at impratio 100, the 1 mm pad's spin-onset arm is 0.787/0.924/1.193 mm against Drake's 0.839/0.987/1.275 mm at 0.5/1/3 N (6.2-6.5 % low), and its arm while spinning at 1 rad/s is within 1.2 % of the integrated pressure law (docs/experiments/20261005-contact_bed/twist_slip.jsonl).
- One pad model gives bed metrics identical to 3-4 digits in CPU MuJoCo, MuJoCo-Warp and Newton (Newton with d0 from its own solver model and its friction gain set for 10 ms), and on the 1 N m plant ends the deployed-plan turn within 3 deg of Drake on 23, 23 and 22 of 24 placements with 40 of 40 held (docs/experiments/20261006-simulator_agreement/).
- MuJoCo's soft friction makes the pads creep under a held load at the speed set by per-sphere dampers c = 2*impratio*k_s*t_c with cone saturation: 22.7/30.8/53.3 um/s at 0.5/1/3 N, predicted and measured to four digits, scaling as 1/impratio from 100 to 10,000 (docs/experiments/20261005-contact_bed/creep.jsonl, pull_slip.jsonl).
- A single point contact cannot hold a pinched tool against gravity: in the hand brake and the pick-brake-insert chain it lets the tool swing free in Drake and MuJoCo, while the 1 mm pads, Drake hydroelastic and condim 4 with load-rescheduled torsion complete both (chain swing ends 90.5, 91.3 and 88.2 deg; one nominal run per model; docs/experiments/20261001-hom_hand_brake, 20261002-hom_chain).
- Newton's SolverMuJoCo path multiplies hydroelastic stiffness by the pair's effective mass: uncorrected, the bed contact sinks 7.1-7.4 times Drake's depth and the hand holds 10 of 40 turns; multiplying k_h by the summed inverse weights gives 1.02-1.04 times Drake's sink with no fit (docs/experiments/20261006-newton_mass_scaling/).
- On one CPU core the pads step 9-86 times faster than Drake hydroelastic: 15.1 against 1,298 us on the sphere bed, 57 against 531 us on the TPU-block bed, 106 against 2,082 us on the full-hand turn replay; they cost 3.1-8.2 times point contact on the hand (105.5 against 13.4 us in the planner grip).
- On the SR2 pinch fixture at 4,096 worlds the pads cost 1.08 us per world-step in MuJoCo-Warp against 1.85 us for mass-corrected Newton hydroelastic with reduced contacts and 4.28 us with all faces; the same pads in Newton cost 1.23 us (docs/experiments/20261005-gpu_scaling/gpu_scaling.jsonl, newton_scaling.jsonl).
- On the bed the pads deviate from Drake by a median 6.2 % against 19.3 % for mass-corrected Newton hydroelastic (about 18 % for Newton at 10-20 ms friction time constants over seven metrics); Newton's reduced contacts reach 0.76-0.88 of Drake's onset torque.
- On the servo-limited plant the fingertip model barely changes the D6 turn: each fingertip is a 250 N/m servo spring, 139 times softer than the pad contact, and six policies hold 1,531 of 1,536 rollouts closed loop and 1,527 when their own actions are replayed on another tip (docs/experiments/20261013-contact_feedback/20261013-contact_parameter_sensitivity.html).
- On the 1 N m working plant with 44-96 N grips, pad and skin policy targets kept the tool in 216 of 220 open-loop replays in CPU MuJoCo, Drake and Newton against 3 of 132 for point-contact policies (docs/experiments/20261008-contact_model_policies/).
- Under a weaker servo the pad policies keep a margin: at half the servo gain (kp 0.36) they hold 256 of 256 closed-loop rollouts against 193 of 256 for TPU-mesh policies (two seeds per arm; docs/experiments/20261011-servo_plant_policies/robust.jsonl).

### Claims that do not survive as stated

- 'The pads reproduce the friction torque and contact area of a soft fingertip' (overview lede): no fingertip has been measured; finite elements of the printed TPU tip put the pads' approach 20-28 % and twist arm 7-23 % off, with a force exponent of 1.07-1.15 against the pads' 1.5.
- The overview's headline numbers (onset 6 % under Drake, arm within 1.2 %, creep 155 times, 15 against 1,298 us) describe the hand's fingertip: they come from the 10.55 mm sphere; on the TPU block used since 13 October the sliding arm is 18-66 % long (4.55 against 3.59 mm at 1 N), the braked tool passes 45 deg at 0.338 against 0.500 N, creep is 9 times Drake's and the cost 9 times below Drake (docs/experiments/20261006-fingertip_backends/tip_T1, tip_T2, tip_T5.jsonl).
- 'Better than Newton hydroelastic' as a property of the methods: every Newton run used reduced contacts without moment matching, the hand turn used a 5 ms friction time constant that overshoots on the bed, and 10 ms rows, moment matching and unreduced contacts were never run on the hand; Newton creeps 3.7-7.0 times less than the pads.
- 'Faster than Newton' on the hand: on the D6 RL state Newton's own pads cost 4.00 us, as much as its hydroelastic contact (4.25 us); the MuJoCo-Warp pads ran on a different build (3.6 against 3.14, and the version alone moves the pads from 2.41 to 1.75 us); the pads used 1.7-2.2 times Newton's GPU memory.
- 'Pad-trained policies transfer across simulators better than point-contact policies': holds only on the 1 N m plant with a grip bonus; on the servo-limited plant replays hold 78 of 88 against 74 of 88 (p = 0.51) and closed-loop transfer is 1,021 of 1,024 against 1,024 of 1,024.
- 'The pads cost 12 % of RL throughput': that is a zero-action benchmark; a PPO iteration costs 1.47-1.82 times the TPU mesh and 11.5-12.5 GB against 2.2-3.3 GB of GPU memory, and the 1 mm pads did not build at 4,096 envs.
- 'Sampling a Winkler foundation with point or sphere contacts is new': Simbody's ElasticFoundationForce (2011), Masterjohn et al. (RA-L 2022), Newton's SDF hydroelastic contact (Rostel et al., 2026) and CSLC (Nechyporenko et al., 2026) precede it.
- Step bound (14), dt < t_c(d0 + (1 - d0)/n): the spring-and-damper bound 2 t_c(sqrt(1+q) - 1) is 5-20 % shorter and matches MuJoCo's spectral threshold to 1e-4 t_c (7.394 against 8.761 ms for the 13-sphere plate pad); point contact fails near d0*t_c, which fits a friction-row bound that neither form gives.
- The 3 N / 0.5 N arm ratio (1.565 foundation, 1.817 Hertz) as the bench test of the printed tip: on the TPU block the arm grows 1.02-1.11 times in finite elements, 1.08 in the pads and 1.05 in Drake.
- 'A 2-5 MPa TPU tip lengthens the arm by 19-50 %': on the block the arm falls 10 % from 5 to 20 MPa (2.66 to 2.39 mm at 1 N); Drake's 10 MPa is a field modulus, and the planned prints fit 6.6-16.5 MPa.
- Creep factor printed as 100, 155 and 177 times Drake's on one page: the pads alone give 155-164 times in translation at a 1 ms step, 34 times at 5 ms and 9 times on the block; 177 pools point-contact rows.
- 'Raising impratio from 100 to 1000 cuts task-6 creep from 16.8 to 1.0 um/s; ten noslip iterations give -0.54 um/s': pooled medians over unlike variants; at 1 N and 1 ms the values are 30.76 to 3.08 um/s, and noslip 3 or 10 gives -1.56 um/s.
- 'A pad contact relaxes in 2 t_c = 20 ms' and 'body_invweight0 is independent of pose': a pushed sphere returns as (1 + t/t_c) exp(-t/t_c), within 5 % after 4.74 t_c = 47 ms, and body_invweight0 is computed once at qpos0.
- 'Planners need the pads in their own rollout model': never tested; every MPC and iCEM run rolled out the plant's own pad model, and point-contact rollouts cost 13.4 against 105.5 us per step.

### Forms of publication and what each needs

- Section and appendix of the hand paper (paper/main.tex): Rerun bed tasks 1, 2, 5 and 8 on the TPU block (pads, TPU mesh, Drake, MuJoCo-Warp pads; 1 and 2 ms steps; impratio 10 and 100; about 1 h on one CPU core and 30 min of GPU) and make them the headline, keeping the sphere as the analytic check. State the calibration d0 = 1 - 1/(t_c^2 K Lambda), the cone-saturated creep law and the corrected step bound 2 t_c(sqrt(1+q) - 1). Give one cost table with bed, hand and RL rows (CPU 57 and 106 us against Drake 531 and 2,082 us; GPU 1.08 against 1.85 us; PPO iteration 1.47-1.82 times the mesh). Report the brake and chain as the torsion tasks and claim agreement with Drake and task completion. A 30 min pull test for mu on the printed tip would add the one measured parameter.
- Four-page workshop paper (contact-rich manipulation or simulation workshop at ICRA, RSS or CoRL): The section's content plus: Newton hydroelastic at its best settings on the bed and on the 24-placement hand turn (10 ms friction time constant, moment matching on, unreduced contacts at 0.25 and 0.5 mm voxels, two GPU passes; about 1 h of GPU); condim 4 with its torsion coefficient from the law integral (0.974 mm N^-1/4) instead of the Drake fit; MuJoCo multiccd on the block mesh as the cheap baseline (bed tasks 2 and 5 at 1 and 5 ms and the turn replay); all GPU timings on one MuJoCo-Warp build; the Newton effective-mass finding. About two weeks, no hardware.
- Standalone letter (RA-L) or full conference paper: The workshop content plus bench measurements on the printed 95A 20 % tip: mu by pull test at 0.5-2 N, twist-onset torque at 1 and 3 N (finite elements predict a 3.12-3.35 mm arm against the pads' 2.72 mm at 3 N), indentation at 0.5/1/3 N (21.5/41.4/114.9 um predicted) and presliding with a 2 um indicator; a reference other than Drake (finite elements or IPC) for twist and brake; CSLC with and without lateral springs; tasks a single contact cannot represent (centre-of-pressure rocking, edge contact, rolling across a pad); and one hardware result the pads change, such as an in-hand pinch brake tracked by the tag or pad-trained against mesh-trained policies on the D6 hand, 20 trials each. Several bench sessions; months.
- Software release (sphere-pad builder with tests): A surface sampler for arbitrary tip meshes with an MjSpec attach in place of the hand-specific builder (body names and block constants are module globals in scripts/fingertip_geometry.py and scripts/reorient_backends.py); guards for the elliptic cone (a pyramidal cone stiffens pads by the impratio), dt <= t_c/2 (refsafe softening), diagexact off and one calibrated partner body (pad contacts with static geoms are 8.9-19.7 times too stiff); the 15 tests ported plus guard tests on MuJoCo 3.6 and 3.14; optionally a per-world d0 re-solve in MuJoCo-Warp for mass randomisation. About 3-4 days of code.
- Upstream Newton issue or pull request: Report that SolverMuJoCo maps hydroelastic k_h into solref without the effective-mass factor (bed sink 7.1-7.4 times Drake's; k_h*(w_tip + w_tool) restores 1.02-1.04 times) and that the MJCF importer's kf 1000 gives a friction time constant of 4e-5 s, with a minimal script derived from scripts/contact_bed_newton.py. A few hours.

## Prior work (literature reader)

Closest prior work: Masterjohn, Guoy, Shepherd and Castro (RA-L 2022), and Röstel et al. (Newton SDF hydroelastic, ICRA 2026 CR2 workshop). Both turn a hydroelastic contact surface into one compliant point contact per polygon, with stiffness g·A and its own Coulomb cone. The friction torque about the normal then comes from the spread of the points, as it does in the pads. The classical ancestors are two:
- Simbody/OpenSim's ElasticFoundationForce: springs k·a at mesh-face centres with k from the layer's E, ν and thickness, and per-spring friction that drifts below stiction.
- Gonthier and McPhee's volumetric contact: spinning friction derived from a Winkler pressure distribution.

The nearest sphere-based relative is CSLC (Nechyporenko, Abderezaei and Roncone, CU Boulder HIRO group, 2026). It adds lateral springs and presliding and costs about 3× point contact. It never tests torsion about the normal.

Known before this work:
- the Winkler arm grows as F^1/4 and the Hertz arm as F^1/3; measured soft-finger exponents run from 0 to 1/3 (Xydas and Kao 1999);
- load-scheduled torsion on a single contact exists in Gazebo/ODE: T = (3π/16)·a·μ·N with a = √(R·d);
- MuJoCo documents slow slippage and its remedies (impratio, noslip);
- solref is mass-normalised (MuJoCo docs; Newton's kh docstring);
- several geoms per fingertip pad are already used (Menagerie Panda: five boxes per finger, uncalibrated).

New as far as this search found:
- a recipe that realises E·a/h per sphere inside MuJoCo's constraint solver with no runtime code, independent of tool mass through body_invweight0, and unchanged in MuJoCo-Warp and Newton;
- the per-row creep formula and the collective step bound for that solver;
- a four-simulator task bed with measured costs;
- the omitted effective-mass factor in Newton's hydroelastic-to-SolverMuJoCo path.

The fast path on both CPU and GPU is real: 15 µs per CPU step against Drake's 1.3 ms, and 1.08 µs per GPU world-step against Newton hydroelastic's 1.85 µs. The edge over Newton is small and depends on settings: Newton ran at commit 009158e6 with moment matching off, 3.9 ms friction rows and a manual mass fix. As a standalone paper this is an engineering note of workshop or RA-L-letter size, not a contact-mechanics contribution. Its value to the owner is a default fingertip model that carries friction torque, has a physical stiffness for co-design, and moves between simulators at 1.7× the mesh tip's RL iteration cost.

A reviewer's first objection would be: this is Drake's per-polygon discretization and the elastic foundation re-implemented in MuJoCo. It is validated only against Drake, which uses the same law and the same unmeasured 10 MPa. Condim 4 fitted to Drake reaches the same median deviation (5.7 % against 6.0–6.2 %) at 10.6 against 15.1 µs per step.

Experiments a reviewer would demand:
1. Torque against normal force, plus indentation, on the printed tip at 0.5–3 N. Winkler predicts an arm ratio of 1.565, Hertz 1.817.
2. An FE or IPC reference on the real 8.8 mm-face block for the twist and the brake.
3. Competitors at their best settings: Newton unreduced with moment matching and 10 ms friction rows; CSLC with and without lateral springs; condim 4 derived from the law, not fitted to Drake.
4. Tasks that a single contact point cannot represent: centre-of-pressure rocking (CSLC's book test), edge contact, rolling across a pad.
5. A hardware comparison of policies trained with pads and with point contact on the servo-limited plant.
6. The dependence of the calibration and the step bound on MuJoCo's discrete integrator and newer versions.

The overview's literature paragraph cites none of Masterjohn 2022, Simbody/OpenSim, Gonthier, Gazebo or the MuJoCo slow-slippage documentation (scripts/contact_overview_text.py lit()). A "Related models" paragraph naming these would let a reader place the method in two minutes.

- Masterjohn, Guoy, Shepherd, Castro, 'Velocity level approximation of pressure field contact patches', IEEE RA-L 7(4), 2022 (2022) https://arxiv.org/abs/2110.04157: Discretizes Drake's hydroelastic contact for velocity-level solvers. Each contact-surface polygon becomes one compliant point contact at its centroid, with stiffness k = g·A (g the series combination g_A·g_B/(g_A+g_B)) and its own Coulomb constraint. A spinning-coin test lands within 1.3 % of the analytic ε* = 0.653. Overlap: The pads' discretization: per-point stiffness equal to pressure gradient times area, a friction cone per point, and friction torque from the spread of points. The Drake reference on the bed uses it. Difference: Pad sites are fixed to the fingertip and their stiffness E·a/h is computed once at load, with no per-step contact-surface extraction and no tetrahedral fields. The stiffness is set through MuJoCo's solref/solimp with the inverse-inertia factor, and the model runs unchanged in MuJoCo, MuJoCo-Warp and Newton.
- Röstel, Liu, Yin, Zamora, Macklin, Reist, Widmer, 'GPU-Accelerated Hydroelastic Contact via Signed Distance Fields', CR2 workshop at ICRA 2026 (local PDF docs/experiments/20261004-codex/11_GPU_Accelerated_Hydroelasti.pdf) (2026) https://openreview.net/forum?id=ogndqznZyY: SDF-based hydroelastic contact in Newton/Warp. Marching cubes on k_A·φ_A − k_B·φ_B gives the patch; each triangle becomes one contact with k_eff = k_A·k_B/(k_A+k_B)·a. Optional reduction keeps the bin wrench and approximately the torsional capacity. Nut and bolt on an RTX 5090: 141× real time reduced, 18× unreduced. No accuracy comparison with Drake. Overlap: The direct GPU competitor: the same per-point stiffness idea and the same SolverMuJoCo path. The paper names MuJoCo's per-geom torsional coefficient as the gap; the pads fill it.
