### deck_vs_overview: Deck 23 of the contact tutorial (soft fingertip contact) against the contact overview docs/overviews/contact_model.html

Deck 23 (58 slides, /home/humanoid/wxie_workspace/contact/tutorial/slides/23_soft_fingertip_contact.html) rebuilds overview steps 1-10 with the algebra the overview skips, and its MuJoCo 3.14 models reproduce the bed to the digit (spinning arm 0.8235/0.9625/1.2674 mm, creep 30.6 um/s at 1 N, task 8 7.09/-7.29 um). Ten derivations are missing or compressed in the overview: the hinge balance 2muF rbar(F) >= mgd cos phi (mgd 3.61 mN m, horizontal hold from 1.64 N per pad), the ring integrals behind 8/15 and 3pi/16, the sagitta and a^2 = 2R delta0 substitution behind F = pi k_w R delta0^2 and the 1/4 power, the torque form of the ratio test, the R - r_s curvature (arm x 0.982), the identity Rf = a_ref - a1 that removes the coupling at rest and gives a transient of time constant t_c, the softest spring 1/(Lambda t_c^2) behind d0 > 0, the friction damper c = 2 kappa k_s t_c with cone saturation, and the explicit spring term in the step bound.

Few of the deck's corrections reached the overview. Eq. (14) is still the damper-only bound (8.761 ms where a plate pad goes unstable at 7.394 ms). The overview still says the contact "relaxes in 2t_c", though it settles in 4.74 t_c, and still calls body_invweight0 pose-independent. It omits the spinning arm's 1 rad/s condition, the 0-8 deg placement spread, the third outcome of the ratio test and the 1.6-2.6 mm patch radius. Drake's 10 MPa field modulus is treated as a TPU Young's modulus. The overview mixes thumb parameters (d0 0.741, Lambda 47.2 1/kg, 205 spheres, 2.83x) with bed measurements (0.866, 90.74 1/kg, 126 spheres). It gives the pad-to-Drake creep ratio as 100x, 155x and 177x; the rows give 155-164x. The bed's creep.jsonl already holds a cone-aware creep prediction that equals the measurement to four digits. Its stability.jsonl holds a friction-row step bound (about d0 t_c) that brackets 18 of 21 pad pairs and all 3 point-contact pairs; both normal-row bounds fail for point contact. Neither page uses these rows. Every pad-versus-Drake number is at impratio 100, while the policies train at 10, where creep is 10x faster. That setting is untested on the bed; the pad's onset arm moves from 5.1 % under the law at 100 to 1.8 % under at 10,000. Three deck statements also need revision. The deck treats the twist test as a measurement of k_w, but a 10 % arm error is a 40 % k_w error. It puts the bed's 19/21 bracketing on rail damping. Its ratio test assumes a spherical tip, while the finite-element arm of the printed TPU block grows only 1.02-1.11x from 0.5 to 3 N. A slide-sync reminder fits in the docstring of scripts/contact_overview_page.py and in the Documentation list of CLAUDE.md.

Verdict: The deck supplies the derivation steps the overview lacks and about a dozen corrections that have not reached it. Propagate the items for steps 2, 6, 8 and 9 first, because they change equations (2), (10), (12) and (14). Label every parameter as thumb or bed. Run gap 1 before rewriting the bed-task-7 paragraph of step 9: the bed's own rows favour a friction-row bound that neither document states.

- Gap: Which rows set the largest stable step of the bed pinch: the overview's eq. (14) (normal-row damper), the deck's 2 t_c (sqrt(1+q) - 1) (normal spring and damper) or the friction rows' damper t_c (d0 + (1-d0) Lambda/(kappa lambda_t)), about d0 t_c. The bed rows already store all three predictions (stability.jsonl); the friction bound brackets 18/21 pad pairs and 3/3 point-contact pairs (2.09, 5.04, 9.01 ms against failures at 3, 7, 10 ms), while both normal-row bounds predict d0-independent 8-10 ms for point contact. Experiment: For the 21 (t_c, d0) pairs of bed task 7 and the 3 point-contact pairs, bisect on the step the spectral radius of mjd_transitionFD about the held pinch (gravity on, 1 N per pad), with rail damping 5 and 0 N s/m and impratio 100 and 10, and record the unstable eigenvector's split between tool tangential translation, tool rotation and pad normal motion. Prediction: the threshold follows pred_dt_max_friction_ms and the mode is tangential; at impratio 10 the point contact's threshold at d0 = 0.2 rises from 2.09 to about 2.9 ms. Falsified if the unstable mode is normal, or if the threshold does not move with impratio as predicted. ### literature_novelty: Prior work and novelty of the sphere-packed fingertip pad contact model

The 1 mm sphere pads are an accurate and cheap discretization of the hydroelastic (Winkler) pressure law inside unmodified MuJoCo. Most of the construction has published precedent. On the two-pad bed the pad's sliding friction arm is within 1.2 % of the sphere–cylinder law integral (0.819, 0.974, 1.279 mm at 0.5, 1 and 3 N). Its spin-onset arm is 3.9–6.8 % below the law and 6.2–6.5 % below Drake, and its slip force is within 0.1 % of Drake's (docs/experiments/20261005-contact_bed/twist_slip.jsonl, pull_slip.jsonl). Solving solimp's impedance from the compiled body_invweight0 makes each sphere a physical spring for tools of 6–98 g: 1,200 N/m at 10 MPa on the TPU block. With a fixed inverse inertia the arm moved by +37/−23 %.

A pad step costs 15.1 µs on one CPU core, against 1,298 µs for Drake. In MuJoCo-Warp at 4,096 worlds it costs 1.08 µs per world-step, against 1.85 µs (reduced) and 4.26 µs (all faces) for Newton's mass-corrected hydroelastic contact. The pads' median deviation from Drake is 6.0–6.2 %, Newton hydroelastic's 19.3 %.

Prior work covers the mechanism:
- Simbody/OpenSim's ElasticFoundationForce (2011) puts a spring k·a with its own friction at every mesh-face centre, and that friction drifts below stiction.
- Drake has turned each pressure-field polygon into one compliant point contact with k = g·A and its own Coulomb cone since Masterjohn et al. (2022).
- Newton passes its SDF triangles to MuJoCo-Warp the same way (Röstel et al. 2026).
- CSLC (Nechyporenko et al. 2026) gives sphere fingertips a distributed patch.
- MuJoCo's documentation states slow slippage and a mass-normalised solref.

The parts specific to this work:
- the MuJoCo parameter mapping;
- the creep formula (0.019/0.028/0.044 mm/s predicted, 0.023/0.031/0.053 measured);
- the collective step bound (19 of 21 pairs bracketed);
- the portability of one model across three simulators;
- the missing effective-mass factor in Newton's hydroelastic path (sink 7.1–7.4× Drake's, 1.02–1.04× after correction).

Every agreement number compares two models that share an unmeasured 10 MPa modulus. The FE of the printed TPU tip puts the pads' twist arm 7–23 % short and the approach 20–28 % off, and the pads creep 155× faster than Drake. No hardware result shows a control benefit. The cross-simulator replay advantage (85/88 against 3/132) was measured at 44–96 N grips on a plant 4.4× too strong. On the servo-limited plant, swapping the tip model changes held rollouts from 1,531 to 1,527 of 1,536.

- Gap: The printed tip's friction torque against normal force has never been measured, so the F^1/4 law, the 10 MPa modulus and the friction arm are untested against hardware. Experiment: Pinch the 12.5 mm screwdriver between two printed 95A 20 % tips at 0.5, 1, 2 and 3 N, with a load cell in the pinch. Spin the tool slowly (about 1°/s) through a reaction-torque sensor and record the torque at spin onset and while sliding, 5 repeats per load. Measure μ with a pull test and E* with an indentation test. Compute the arm τ/(μN) and the ratio arm(3 N)/arm(0.5 N): Winkler gives 1.565, Hertz 1.817, and the FE gives 3.20 mm at 3 N. The pad law is falsified if the ratio lies outside 1.45–1.70 or if the arm at the indentation-fitted E* differs by more than 15 % at any load. Reuse: scripts/tpu_tip_calibrate.py; the bed task-2 protocol in scripts/contact_bed_twist.py for the matched simulation; FE rows in docs/experiments/20261009-tpu_tip_fem/; print files in docs/experiments/20261009-tpu_tip_fem/print/ Compute: Half a bench day; the matched simulation takes minutes on one core Hardware: True
- Gap: The model is validated on a sphere tip (R 10.55 mm), but the deployed tip is the 8.8 mm-face TPU block with 2.7 mm fillets. On that block the pads miss the FE twist arm by 7–23 % and the approach by 20–28 %. Experiment: Run bed tasks 2 (twist) and 5 (brake) with the tpu2.7 pads at each print's E* (6.9–16.5 MPa). Extend the FE twist arm to 0.5 and 1 N; only 3 N is reported now. Test two cheap fixes: a normal spring under the pad face fitted to the FE approach (exponent 1.07–1.15), and a refit foundation depth h. Keep a fix if the arm error at 0.5, 1 and 3 N stays under 10 % and the approach error under 10 %; otherwise the sampled foundation cannot represent the printed tip. Settings: moment_matching=True; reduction on and off; voxels of 0.25 and 0.5 mm; friction rows matched to 10 ms; kh multiplied by the pair's effective mass. Report the median deviation from Drake and µs per world-step at 1k–8k worlds. The pads' advantage is falsified if unreduced, moment-matched Newton comes within 3 points of the pads' deviation at no more than 1.5× their per-world-step cost (1.6 µs). Report the effective-mass omission upstream with the bed numbers. - Gap: Condim 4 with a torsion coefficient fitted to Drake matches the pads' median deviation (5.7 % against 6.0–6.2 %) at 10.6 against 15.1 µs per step. No task yet shows where the spheres are needed. Experiment: (a) Run condim 4 with μ_t from the law integral (C_LAW 0.974e-3, exponent 1/4, no Drake fit) and with Gazebo's Hertz schedule a = √(R·d), T = (3π/16)·a·μ·N, on all bed tasks and the D1–D8 replays. (b) Add tasks that a single contact point cannot represent: CSLC's book test (0.3 kg, 2×10×20 cm, μ = 1, a 3 N pulse for 50 ms normal to the face); twist with the tool edge on the pad; rolling the tool across the pad's edge. The pads are justified if condim 4 deviates by more than 10 % from the pads and Drake, or drops the tool where they hold it. Experiment: Deploy the servo-plant finals on the D6 hand at the bench: pads seeds 0 and 1, TPU-mesh seeds 0 and 1, 20 trials each, tracked by AprilTag. Record held rate and held cosine at 5 s. The claim that pads improve control is falsified if the pad and mesh arms differ by less than the seed spread (0.09 in held cosine) and the held rate by fewer than 3 of 20. MuJoCo's discrete integrator corrects the diagonal per row and folds the spring–damper into the end-of-step impedance.
